# Gender Capacity in AFD, PROPARCO and GIZ

The following paragraph explains with detail the capacity of AFD to manage and monitor the gender-related activities at project level and the particular tools and experiences of PROPARCO and GIZ that will generate important synergies to succeed during implementation.

AFD has vast experience with large portfolios of projects with the mandate to include gender equality and women's empowerment. Since 2013, AFD defined its strategy on Gender and the Reduction of Gender Inequalities (GRI) as a key element in its development cooperation. The GRI aims at increasing gender equality and women’s empowerment and support in societies on these issues. Today, its objectives are more ambitious: to achieve gender equality by 2030. AFD has three project categories based on the classification of OECD DAC markers: DAC1: the project does not target gender equality; DAC2: gender equality is the main objective but it is a difficult and deliberate objective of the project; DAC3: gender equality is an important and deliberate objective of the project. The DAC2 category projects are those that explicitly achieve the objective of 50 percent of the volume of commitments to be gender-responsive and rated 1 or 2 on the Gender Equality Index (GEI) of the World Bank Group.

Moreover, AFD manages a Support Fund for Feminist Civil Organizations aiming at building the organizational, financial and technical capacities of feminist organizations in partner countries and promoting their participation in decision-making processes at national, sub-regional, regional and local levels. Together with 26 banks, AFD signed a Paris development bank statement on gender equality in 2015. The statement commits to three main actions: (i) Strengthen the commitment of Public Development Banks to gender equality through a dedicated institutional strategy; (ii) Increase the share of financial flows for gender-responsive operations; (iii) Contribute to the implementation and monitoring of gender equality strategies, in close collaboration with all stakeholders on gender issues, including feminist civil society organizations. AFD’s experience in the region and long term collaboration with regional and local agencies will facilitate the dialogue with public transportation authorities.

PROPARCO belongs to the 2X Challenge and brings gender-lens investing experience to support the project(s) opportunities to increase women's economic agency. PROPARCO (Société de Promotion et Participation à la Coopération économique) is a subsidiary of AFD and as part of AFD Group follows its gender strategy. It aims at increasing women’s access to financial services, markets, land tenure, education, equity and quasi-equity and support (technical assistance and expertise) to both businesses and financial intermediaries in order to increase their capacity to provide products and services that meet the needs of female entrepreneurs. In 2018, PROPARCO implemented 35 projects in 14 countries. The share of infrastructure projects represents more than 30% of PROPARCO’s activities in these countries. PROPARCO mobilizes its funds to increase women's participation in development countries through promoting women's financial inclusion and access to markets. It does so by focusing on two main areas: (i) Financial inclusion, which is widely adopted by other development institutions; PROPARCO’s criteria to achieve gender equality are four: 1) Entrepreneurship, access to financing for women-owned and operated businesses; 2) Leadership and decision-making in the private sector; 3) Access to markets and trade; 4) Essential goods and services, access to goods and services benefiting women in particular. PROPARCO will implement the Gender Assessment Tool (GAT) as a tool to assess gender equality at project level and its mandate and know-how to mainstream gender in its activities will facilitate women's inclusion.

GIZ is a pioneer organization working on gender and transportation. In 2007 GIZ published the toolkit "Gender and Urban Transport", which has been a must-have tool for development practitioners for more


---
**Page 178**
---

than a decade and has been updated in 2018 (TUMI: Transformation Urban Mobility initiative, implemented by GIZ) launched an international conference "Women Making Mobility" which in 2018 shaped a new narrative around gender and transportation and triggered follow up initiatives and networks of female change makers from all over the world. One of the regional networks on gender and mobility is the E-mobility Action Plan (EAP) for Gender Equality, which aims to increase women's decision making, planning and operation of transport. GIZ experience in transport will accelerate the current tendency to include gender and vulnerable groups and this work in its facility will multiply the synergies amongst local and regional actors.

# Recommendations

In these seven countries, gender equality has been improving in recent years, but the COVID-19 pandemic has worsened it. The country snapshots on gender equality of the numerous initiatives are enough to recommend that a national action plan for this facilitator and gender strategy should be included in action plans to prevent violence against women and girls and guarantee women's access to direct jobs and economic opportunities by the projects receiving loans.

The second section of the assessment reveals that these countries are already implementing programs and actions related to gender equality, including gender equality. The E-mobility and low carbon Transportation facility is a timely opportunity to build from many initiatives already in place. The TA will house all the activities related to gender equality and women's empowerment. The efforts to establish a sustainable framework for gender equality and women's empowerment are an important addition which will multiply the effects of the gender-related activities in the new paradigm. The outcomes and activities of the Gender Action Plan are shaped in line with the facility’s.

The technical assistance will support the adopters implement the gender centered design. Adding to the differentiation of the EAP, the TA will include the differentiators detailed in the gender assessment. The adopters will be guided on how to apply intersectionality enablers. Adding to the afo descendants and indigenous peoples present in many of these countries, intersecting categories of age, social economic status, work type, etc., will be considered when designing products and services. In addition to being compliant with universal design approach, accessibility and reasonable accommodations[1] will be factored in when electrifying public transport systems. The TA will also include a gender equality action plan to guarantee that women and vulnerable groups are represented during multi-stakeholders consultations so that their perspectives can be factored into policies, road maps, training and campaigns. The adopters should use tools such as participatory design workshops, focus group discussions, interviews, surveys, and private to allow women and vulnerable groups to share their perspectives. An environment where women and vulnerable groups feel safe will allow the adopters to include perspectives during design and implementation that might not be considered otherwise. Consultations in a safe environment will be

[1] UN Women, UNDP, and more (2021). Intersectionality resource guide and toolkit. An intersectional approach to leave no one behind.

Universal design means the design of products, environments, programmes and services to be usable by all people, to the greatest extent possible, without the need for adaptation or specialized design. Universal design does not preclude devices for particular groups of persons with disabilities where this is needed. Reasonable accommodation refers to adjustments in policies, practices, procedures and conditions that are reasonable or undue burden, where needed in a particular case, to ensure to persons with disabilities the enjoyment or exercise on an equal basis with others of all human rights and fundamental freedoms.

particularly emphasized when designing and implementing sexual harassment programs and prevention campaigns.

The review of public policies at country level will integrate gender lens, this means that the technical assistance will help find obstacles and risks in the policies being reviewed that may impede women from benefiting from the EV mass adoption. The themes covered by the topics in section two of this assessment (mobility, safety, and jobs) constitute a guide that the technical assistance will use when providing support to the implementation of the gender lens. The technical assistance will provide guidance on how to provide assistance to systematically include a gender assessment and gender action plan with concrete measures, activities and programs for women in the public policies being reviewed. This will be done by identifying direct jobs. The technical assistance will help identify the potential sexual harassment risks to consider when designing and implementing e-mobility transport systems. The adopters will receive guidance to develop mechanisms that allow them to address these risks and prevent sexual harassment from occurring. The objective of these technical assistance is that the adopters include Sexual Harassment Programs that contain safe, confidential and ethical grievance mechanisms for survivors of sexual harassment and GBV. These programs must be designed in a way that they are not only effective but also culturally sensitive as well. Moreover, these programs must prevent further risks such as victim blaming and discrimination and mitigate potential impacts on indigenous peoples and Afro-descendants and other vulnerable identities.

Specific actions to guarantee women’s safe when using the E-Vs: As women are and as users, more susceptible in the region. Gender-based violence is endemic among the victims and underreported. Women in the region are victims in the transport sector, and the COVID-19 pandemic is exacerbating the number of incidents. The urban transport system in the region is characterized by a high level of sexual harassment that occurs quotidianly in the regular trips women do every day. Incidents will happen in the e-buses, in the e-taxis, while waiting, walking to and from the stops or using charging stations. Incidents increase at night, in isolated places, and when women are alone. The region has a high number of incidents that affect mostly women but all the users. The recent study “Ella se mueve seguro” (She’s moving safely) represents a timely and adequate guide from the region that will be used by the program to build capacity and aim at project level, through technical assistance, to provide guidance on how to design, implement, monitor, evaluate, and prevent sexual harassment in the public transport sector. The purpose of the South-to-South learning visit is to share lessons learned with a response program that includes grievance mechanisms.

Women are not discriminated in access to credit in any of the seven countries. The program promotes women’s empowerment by providing investments loans to entry into the EV market transport in order to prevent leaving them behind. Women will benefit from the capacity building and training on EV and driving that will be delivered by the facility.

Women are not represented in the jobs created by the project. The proportion of women who lost their jobs and those whose core responsibilities have increased. This facility is an opportunity for women in these seven countries to access the jobs generated by the projects to be designed with the investment loans. For instance, the public sector projects will provide opportunities for women to work as drivers in the e-buses, e-taxis and e-wats will create. This can be done via scholarships for women to receive trainings on professional driving. The technical assistance will also support investment projects for jobs adoption on how to integrate gender equality into the project design and implementation. Women are not represented in the job creation by the project.

region. The technical assistance will help the adopters to consider vulnerable groups who do not have access to technology.

To prevent female turnover and retain women workers in the projects, an activity is focused on the promotion of gender balance for the projects. The activity will include workshops that cover practical examples to learn how GBV, Sexual harassment and other barriers affect women's participation in the work place, with concrete examples on the case of intersectionalities. The aim is for the adopters to understand the importance of having a gender equality policy and to develop one if they do not have it at the organization to monitor and report advances in gender equality and inclusive environments in the organization. Paternalism and gender biases, lack of day care facilities, pay gap, wage gaps, recruitment and promotion criteria for women's advancement in the sector and the need of diversity working environments will be covered within these workshops. A gender focal point will be appointed by each organization to monitor and report on progress towards the target: report gender equality in the human resources policies and act as a sexual harassment ombudsman.

Data collection is crucial to learn, share and improve transport systems, an activity will help transport systems to use disaggregate data systematically in order to inform public transport agencies on how to improve their services. The indicators that will be determined before specific projects are financed in order to monitor and measure the indicators:

The number of expected households (male and female in each country) with improved access to low emission energy sources depends on each country's energy policy, and the number of people able to access to e-mobility services will depend on the availability of charging points. The number of female passengers who will use low-carbon transport as a result of funding support in the different countries will depend on both the lines to be electrified and the personal choice that determine a modal change.

The Gender Action Plan guarantees that these general recommendations are transformed into specific activities by the facility, and that they have a budget and are measured and monitored, and adjusted when needed.

# Gender Action Plan

## E-Mobility and Low Carbon Transportation

| Indicators and Targets | Timeline | Responsibilities | Costs in EUR |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Impact Statement:** Women experiences and benefits from the Transport Sector are improved<br><br>**Outcome Statement:** The targeted transportation sub-sectors are more gender-responsive and provide equal opportunities for men and women<br><br>**Output 1:** Gender considerations to be included during the revision of roadmaps and policies (National level)<sup>[2]</sup> |  |  |  |
| **Activity 1.1. Usage of a gender perspective including mainstreaming of the same when designing and reviewing roadmaps and policies for different genders**<br><br>- Sector specific and country-level gender assessments undertaken as part of the development of low carbon vehicle roadmaps are to be developed<br>Target: 1 per country | During year 1–3, on a rolling basis | GIZ Gender Specialist | **116,800** |
| - If all the policies and roadmaps supported that include a gender perspective<br>- Changes in policies and roadmaps supported by the Program are reviewed and gender equality considerations adopted<br><br>- Users' satisfaction of new PT environment, disaggregated by sex (% very satisfied, satisfied, dissatisfied) | Year 5 |  |  |

<sup>[1]</sup> See disaggregated data will be reported for all the activities in the program of the program. Based on those, information on contextualized gendered needs of different groups of users and their access to transport services in the public transport sector will be provided.<br><sup>[2]</sup> The program will support the design of new roadmaps and policies and will review current policies to recommend how to adapt them to create a mobility conducive environment for all genders. This will include, but not limited to, gender equality considerations. In particular, policies will be reviewed following the differences found in the assessment in terms of mobility, safety and jobs. Moreover, these reviews will identify sexual harassment risk, propose response, grievance and monitoring mechanisms, and consider intersectionality.

## Activity 2.1. To increase awareness about gender equality gaps and opportunities in EV sector
### % of capacity building activities (i.e. webinars, workshops, publications) of the facility that include awareness on gender equality and urban transport* Target: 80%
### % of participants of the exchange platform attending trainings and capacity building activities on EV that are women Target: 30% - 40%

| Year 1 - 5 | GIZ | 110,400 |
| --- | --- | --- |

* This activity guarantees that gender equality is learned and integrated into the activities to be carried out by the facility related to capacity building. It is expected that in some capacity building activities it will not be feasible to integrate gender aspects. Besides, this activity will foster and monitor women's participation on the capacity building activities, making it mandatory to reach a minimum of 30 percent women attending. These activities will consider accessibility and reasonable accommodations to allow women with disabilities or other needs to participate in these activities.
* A target between 30%-40% aims at ensuring that women at least constitute a «critical minority». Targeting a 30% of women to participate is the critical mass needed to guarantee that they are members of this platform in a sector which is traditionally masculine. A parity of 50% is very difficult to achieve and it is not realistic, in a traditional context.
* The satisfaction surveys in the activities will include a question to measure the quality of the content on gender and also seeks to find out if the content needs to be adjusted.

## Output 3: Integrate gender components in the projects financed by the Program

The online module will have verification means of attendance. The training will be practical with initiatives on the region (no theoretical), 3 hours max per day.

Module on mobility (1 day), Module on jobs (2 days), 1 module on how to prepare a gender action plan (1 day). The content will cover tools like interactive identites in all the modules.
The facilitator will be trained in the use of the online format and will have access to the platform/any investment to receive it. At this stage is not possible to know the number of participants. The online format allows the content to be used multiple times.

This public communication campaign will be developed with a focus on the different identities that people face, with significant attention to indigenous peoples and also-discrimination potential risks. The protocols of attention will develop/enhance safe, confidential, and ethical grievance reporting mechanisms for all users of the new transport system. This will allow the new operators to have more tools to help the adopters to develop protocols of attention that are inclusive and safe for different identities present in each country/city of the new e-transport system and will benefit from the implementation of technologies, such as panic buttons or telephone applications.

1 The adopters will receive guidance on developing clear mechanisms of identifying, responding, and addressing risks of sexual harassment. These reporting systems will be built from existing mechanisms already in the cities when available and plan to engage different local actors such as the police. The adopters will receive guidance to enhance safe, confidential, and ethical grievance reporting mechanisms for survivors and monitoring mechanisms.

2 Target: 100% of adopters will include workshops that cover practical examples to learn how GBV, Sexual harassment and other barriers affect women’s participation in the work place, with concrete examples on the case of international organizations. The workshops will also include sessions on how to monitor and report advances in gender equality and inclusive environments in the organization. Paternity and maternity leaves, availability of day care, lactation rooms, wage gaps, and other issues will be covered as part of the training and will be included in the curriculum. A gender focal point will be appointed to advance the work-life balance agenda of the adopters, to monitor and report gender equality in the human resources policies and not act as a sexual harassment ombudsman. A model of survey will be shared in the workshop to be used by the agency/company adopter of the technology to allow adopters to use it regularly with their workforce.
